ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u17	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I present a report by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on a draft government law amending the Act on the Social Security System and some other laws, print No 1188. The Marshal of the Sejm, in accordance with the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, sent, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, on 19 May 2021 the above draft law... [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] [[Chairman of the proceedings includes Deputy Marshal Małgorzata Kidawa-Błońska Sejm]] I would very much like to ask Members who are not taking part in this part of the sitting to leave the Chamber and to allow the rapporteur to provide a statement of reasons. Thank you. Thank you very much, Marshal. On 19 May 2021, the above draft law was first read by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. The aim of the project is to organise and rationalise the social security system, including the introduction of uniform arrangements for the granting and payment of benefits, as well as the improvement of the functioning of the Social Insurance Institution in the context of the financial economy, and the settlement of contributions with contributors. At the stage of application of the existing rules, there were also differences in interpretation which the project eliminates. The aim is to harmonise the rules on social security coverage of social security entitlements and their payment. And yes: standardization of the rules governing the insurance cover of members of single-member companies of o.o. and the shareholders of a public partnership, partnership company or limited partnership company - the current legal state raises doubts of interpretation in relation to persons engaged in activities which are partners of companies: public, limited partnership or partnership, and a single member company.; it is proposed to make the social security obligation dependent on the status of the partner; to harmonise the rules for the payment of sickness benefits in the social security and supply system so-called. uniform services; harmonising the rules of procedure for the right to sickness benefits from accident and sickness insurance; simplifying the rules for voluntary insurance; aligning the forms of payment of long-term benefits; the right to deduct unpaid contributions from benefits paid by the undertaking and to deduct pension benefits from the benefits paid; and the clarification of the rules on the possibility to claim the benefits unduly collected; simplification of the procedure for documenting the right to parental supplementary benefits; electronicisation of applications for certificates A1. The Act also includes changes in the determination of the right to benefits. And so, another way of valorizing contributions and initial capital for people who apply for retirement in June. It's a very expected change. It is proposed that the 2020 solution on the rules for determining the amount of new pensions granted in June, from 2021 onwards, including the valorisation of initial capital and the contributions recorded in the insured account should be implemented permanently, in the same way as in determining the amount of the pension in May, if this is more favourable to the insured person. It is proposed to increase the scope of pension protection for persons who have become completely incapable of working during the period of taking care, special care allowance or care allowance or after the right to these benefits has ceased. It is proposed to harmonise the rules, inter alia. as regards the conversion of pensions from so-called the old and new pension scheme continuing to work; the amendment of the rules on the suspension of social pensions in the event of the exercise of professional activity; the change in the right to sickness insurance benefits. The provisions contained in the Act are intended to improve the handling and orderly the actions of the payers in order to stabilise the balance of the accounts of payers and insured persons. It is proposed to limit the transmission of corrections to documents insured up to the age of 5; changes in the area of remission of claims for non-counting interest contributions for reasons attributable to the undertaking and the issue of certificates of non-legitimate status. It is proposed to modify the concept of "type of business' according to PKDs, as well as the obligation for all contributors to establish a PUE. As regards the improvement of the handling of settlements with contributors, it is proposed to introduce a provision clearly defining what events to differentiate the interest rate of the accident insurance contribution are considered to be an accident at work and therefore what accidents the employer should include in the information of the IWA ZUS. There is no succession of the interest rate of the accident insurance contribution in cases of transformation, merger or division of the contributors. It is proposed to clarify how the payer's accident insurance contribution rate is determined during the transitional period, i.e. until a certificate of entry in the REGON register containing information on the activity by PKD is obtained from the statistical office. It also proposes changes in the scope of the financial economy carried out by the Social Security Office. And yes, it is proposed that the reserve fund be terminated. Ensure the proportionality of the revenue and costs of the FUS. Mode of making and approving changes to the financial plan of the undertaking. It is proposed to introduce the possibility of redemption, deferral or relief in the payment of civil claims by the Social Insurance Institution. New rules for the appointment of the Supervisory Board of the Social Insurance Institution are proposed. Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! The Social Policy and Family Committee, after first reading and considering this draft law at its meeting on 27 May 2021, invites the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u18	Thank you very much, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Anna Dąbrowska-Banaszek, Law and Justice will be the first to speak. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u19	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the government draft law amending the Act on the social security system and certain other laws, print Nos 1188 and 1212. The aim of the project has already been mentioned by the rapporteur. Now I'm gonna pass on the major shifts. The bill includes, among others, the following proposals. Uniformisation of the rules governing the coverage of members of single-member limited-liability companies and members of a public, partnership or limited partnership company. The social security obligation will arise from the date on which the companies are incorporated into the National Court Register or from the date on which they acquire participation in the company, and will cease from the date on which the company is removed from the National Court Register or the sale of all shares in the company, excluding the period of suspension of the company's activities. The next proposal is to simplify the rules for voluntary insurance. Submitting to voluntary insurance will take place from the date indicated in the application form until the date indicated in the registration form. At the same time, the inclusion or termination of voluntary insurance may take place no earlier than the relevant notification, the registration will be filed with the Social Security Office. The notified person will be subject to this insurance even if the contribution is paid after the deadline or is not paid at all. It will also be possible to apply for sickness insurance benefits if contributions are paid after the deadline and for persons whose incapacity to work occurs during the period during which they are overdue for contributions, the newly introduced regulations will allow for the application for benefits after payment of the debt on an equivalent basis as for accident insurance benefits. The deduction from paid claims for contributions to benefits paid by the Social Security Office will be carried out by minimising the costs of the investigation. Now there are about 170,000 cases like this. for the amount of nearly PLN 170.200 million. Clarify the provisions on the possibility to investigate unduly collected benefits. In the case of the performance of work during the period of the declared incapacity to work or the use of dismissal in a manner incompatible with the purpose of that exemption, the Social Security Office shall issue decisions refusing entitlement to the benefit and requiring the reimbursement of the wrongly collected allowance. Changes in the forms of payment of long-term benefits. From 1 January 2022, these benefits will be provided in non-cash form or through service providers. However, it is proposed to leave the current regulation for short-term benefits without change - it is about sickness, maternity and caring benefits, rehabilitation benefits. In addition, the right to sickness benefits. There will be new rules for calculating periods of incapacity for work up to one allowance period. It will include both periods of continuous incapacity for work and periods of incapacity for work that occurred before and after the break, if this interruption is no longer than 60 days and if this incapacity does not occur during pregnancy. The length of the allowance period during the insurance period will not change - it is still, in principle, 182 days and 270 days in case of incapacity during pregnancy and tuberculosis. In contrast, the period of sickness allowance after the end of the insurance period will be reduced - it has been assumed that it will not be longer than 91 days. The new rules will concern the appointment of the Supervisory Board of the ZUS. These rules will apply from the end of the current 5-year term of office of the Supervisory Board, i.e. from October 2022 The obligation to establish a PUE ZUS by all contributors. Currently, employers employing up to five people are exempted from the obligation to establish a ZUS information profile. According to the new rules, from 1 January 2023 everyone will have to do it. The Law and Justice Club supports a procedated bill. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u20	Thank you very much, Member. Please, Mrs Joann Frydrych, the Civic Coalition. You're welcome, my fellow Member.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u21	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Coalition is pleased to present the position of the club on the draft law amending the Act on social security and certain other laws. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! This draft law introduces changes in the scope of e.g.: rules on the granting of pensions in June, rules on the payment of sickness benefits, rules on the application of social insurance to persons who are members of a public partnership, limited partnership or partnership company, rules on the conversion of pensions from so-called. an old and new system of pensioners continuing their professional activity and the liquidation of the reserve fund. The other changes are of a sequencing nature. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! This law provides for the reduction of the allowance period after the end of the title of insurance from 182 days to 91 days. Did the designers consult the medical services? Is it possible to cure the depression caused by the termination of the employment relationship within 3 months? The Act also provides for periods of incapacity to work after a break not exceeding 60 days from the last incapacity to work to include those periods in one allowance period, even if the incapacity to work before the break and after the break was caused by another disease unit. Now imagine the situation: an employee became incapable of working because of a femur fracture - a complicated disease - used a full allowance of 182 days, and after a month it turned out to be a cancer. Ladies and gentlemen, this person will no longer benefit from the right to sickness benefits because the government today wants to push through restrictions that shorten the allowance periods. This provision is extremely reckless and focused only on savings. On behalf of the Civic Coalition Club, I will make an amendment to this matter. Another shift. The Act covers the shareholders of a single-member company with an o.o. and the partners of the limited partnership, the public and partnership obligation to be subject, to pay social security contributions. Did the government ever think that in time after the pandemic, many companies are bounced back after the crisis, and you want to charge them extra costs? What will the health contribution look like when the New Deal is introduced? You are talking more and more about civic retirement, in the National Reconstruction Plan you are talking about longer working lives. Ladies and gentlemen, let the government finally take up the debate, invite experts, take advantage of science and research and propose something clear and clear to the citizen. I also make an amendment here. With regard to the new rules for granting pensions to persons whose applications were submitted to the Social Security Office in June, this is a good solution. However, the Act does not provide for the possibility of converting according to new rules for persons who have been entitled to a pension in June 2009 to 2019. The provision is non-constitutional and does not comply with the principle of equal treatment. I therefore make an amendment on the possibility of converting June pensions under the new rules for persons who acquired the right to retire in June 2009 to 2019. This amendment will meet the principle of equal treatment and pensioners will have a sense of justice. The proposed amendments provide for the harmonisation of the possibility of converting pensions granted from the old system and the new system. These changes will result in a working pensioner who retires from the old system being able to count his benefits once a year and not, so far, once a quarter, or four times a year. These changes are detrimental to active pensioners. At this point the question arises: Does the government seek to increase pensions for active pensioners, or will it block them from increasing benefits? Thus, they are discouraged from being active and paying social security contributions. We will also make an amendment here. The proposed bill provides for the liquidation of the reserve fund. This fund is created as a reserve fund for funds: pensions, sickness and accident, and is created by the Social Security Office from funds remaining on 31 December each year on the Social Insurance Fund bank account. The reason for this change is that there is a continuing shortage of funds in the Social Insurance Fund and there is a need to subsidise this fund from the State budget. [[♪ The bell ♪]] But looking at the financial statements, you can see that every year about PLN 2 billion remains in the FUS account. In my view, the liquidation of this fund will obscure transparency. We will also make an amendment on this issue. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club will support this bill, but provided that our amendments are adopted. Otherwise, we'll vote against. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The reasons for the government project show the types of these benefits, e.g. those introduced for mothers 4+, less than 37% use the banking system. We must take into account that many of the recipients are independent, living in small, rural environments where there is no Internet access. Today, to say that they must be forced to accept their constitutional right to retirement by having this account and accounting, we find it inappropriate. Resignation from mandatory delivery of administrative decisions in the mass process is justified when benefits, as for example. The 13th pension is granted to all alike. But when we have a one-time benefit, so-called. The 14th pension, differentiated, should then also be provided at the request of the insured person himself. The first misunderstanding - I am immediately denouncing all these statements - concerns the issue of banking. There are no amendments to the Act as regards the payment of pension benefits. The amendment applies only to non-cash payments intended for persons residing outside the country. Only in this respect is a change envisaged. There are no changes in the case of persons residing in the country. Today, by filling out a print on retirement or pension, we will mark whether we want to use a bank account - then we need to enter an account number - whether we want to use mail service, and of course then the delivery will be delivered to the postman, as it is today. There is therefore no change in this respect. The second very important issue concerns pensions in June. According to the June pension estimates, in the last 11 years the number of people who retired in June amounted to 124 000. Let me remind you that the issue of June pensions arose in 2009, because then these changes were introduced, and today we are trying to sort it out. Already last year we introduced a single change related to COVID so that benefits are calculated on the same basis as the rules applied in other months, and now we want to introduce this systemically. Of course, there has been a proposal that we should even up the amounts for these 11 years, but we have not decided to do so. Of course, this is related to the question of the resources we would have to have for this solution, and this is the amount of more than PLN 4 billion. We calculated in advance after the discussions in committee that this would be the cost of the change. If the Constitutional Tribunal finds that this is not in accordance with the Constitution, then, of course, we will be working together to figure out how to resolve this matter. Today we have proposed a solution that will apply to people who are retiring and pensioning already this year, i.e. if someone retires this month - in June - it will also have a calculated benefit according to the new rules, even if the law enters into force after this month. That's how we solve this case. As I said, the determinant is 2009 when it comes to issues related to June pensions. As regards issues relating to the sickness fund and the changes that we are proposing, of course, we need these provisions, i.e. the changes that we are making when it comes to the payment of sickness benefits, to read together. 182 days are counted together, this is a fundamental change. Of course, when a woman is pregnant, we do not change the rules, the break must be at the level of 60 days so that another right to sickness can be acquired. Today, there were situations where someone received another 180 days' sick leave before the 180th day. On the other hand, we propose a change in the introduction of a period of 91 days after the end of insurance. And in one case, and in the other case after that period, if the right to sickness benefit ends, the person may benefit from a rehabilitation benefit paid for a year, which allows him to return to work. A very important issue, nothing to hide, is the financial issue when it comes to the sickness fund. I'll give you the details of the expenditure from the sickness fund over the past few years. 2016 - 19 billion PLN, 2019 - 23 billion PLN, 2020 - 29 billion PLN. This last year is special because of COVID. In terms of sickness benefits - here the numbers are impressive - in 2016 we spent more than PLN 10 billion on sickness benefits paid by the Social Insurance Institution, we are talking about this part, which is also paid by employers, in 2020 - PLN 14 billion. These are data that indicate that we need to make changes in this system. As regards the absence of sickness, since 2017, the number of sick leave days paid from the Social Insurance Fund has been almost 150 million per year. In 2016 - 146 million days, in 2019 - 143 million days, in 2020 - 154 million days. This is a huge increase. Interesting data, which is worth mentioning here, show that in 2020 medical sick leave was mainly issued for a period of between 11 and 30 days, almost 40% the total redundancies. So here, too, during the mentioned period, the period of sick leave was extended. This is one of the information we need to learn from. The question raised the issue of medical dismissals for mental disorders and behavioral disorders, among others. for depression. There is also a huge increase in this respect compared to previous years, and the period of exemption is also prolonged, hence the changes that arise for different reasons. We are also trying to make changes in this respect. As far as the issue of limited partnership is concerned, we are not making any changes here. These changes are also dictated by the case law of the Supreme Court and by the judgment of the Constitutional Court, which in its case law states that we need to clarify the rules so that they are clear in resolving various types of disputes. Such solutions are here in this regard. Mrs Bartus asked about the whole amendment of the law or the new draft law. Of course, we will systematically pass to Parliament further draft laws on automation, i.e. those measures which are intended to significantly improve the functioning on the part of the Social Insurance Institution, but also the entrepreneurs and payers, so that these actions are consistent and to put an end to the irregularities we are talking about here. The situation with retirement is that if someone meets conditions in Poland today, i.e. when a woman is 60 years old and a man 65 years old, it is in the current pension system that there is no possibility of refusing to grant a pension. Just work one day and that's the case in this case. [[...under special conditions.]] Unless there are special conditions, this is a different situation, here the Member is right. In this case, it is necessary to check whether this period, e.g. 15 years under harmful conditions is documented. In such situations there may be confusion, and therefore in the Social Insurance Institution there are so-called. Retirement advisers who can help, because we know that in such a case, in order to retire, you have to terminate your employment contract and that's why there are sometimes problems that are later resolved on a judicial basis. The Member also asked about the ZUS and KRUS benefits. These are two benefits. The witnessing from the CSO can be purchased at any time and from the CRUS under certain conditions. As far as matters related to the supervisory board are concerned, there is no attempt on the employers' side. Until now, there has been a consensus in the Supervisory Board, i.e. there is a balance between trade union representatives and employers' organisations, plus there is always an organisation of pensioners and government party. That was the supervisory board's arrangement. But over the years, four additional representative employers' organisations have been established in Poland, so employers' organisations also have their representative under the law, trade unions are at the same level, that is, trade unions are representative. There were two solutions to equalize - so to speak - the same level of representation of employers and trade unions. You can either raise the number of members of trade union organisations, but this is the way to nowhere, because there may be other organisations, or you can just put in an equal number. We have decided that for all sides there will be equal numbers, so there will be three representatives of the union side, three representatives of employers, i.e. out of this group must emerge themselves, the party of pensioners always had one representative and so will remain, and three representatives of the government side, that will be a total of 10 people. We've also introduced a provision that says that the current council in the old lineup operates until the end of October 2022, so we will not make any changes in the process here. In conclusion, as I said, the main objective is to rationalise expenditure, but also to improve the situation of people who use the services of the Social Insurance Institution. There is no temptation here to make anything difficult or even difficult - as sharp words have been - to take something away from someone. We're not taking it. We're rationalizing this system. As far as the old and new system is concerned, of course, we maintain here that it will be possible to recalculate pensions. We are evening out, so that the old and the new system will make the conversion of pensions on the same basis. Previously, in the old system, there were more favourable terms of conversion than in the current system, so this issue is settled. I have tried to answer these questions here. One of the Members asked about the status of the Social Insurance Fund. I was talking about the sickness fund, and as far as the Social Insurance Fund is concerned, expenditure and the fund are at the level of about PLN 300 billion - it is two hundred and ninety several billion PLN - and the grant from the state budget is at the level of over PLN 40 billion. Each year, when discussing the state budget, these data are provided by the Social Insurance Institution. Yesterday, the Social Insurance Institution also presented this information from the implementation of the 2020 budget at the meeting of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs. There are also detailed, detailed data presented by the Social Insurance Institution. That's it. Once again, thank you very much for this discussion and I ask you to pass the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u121	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Gentlemen of Ministers! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Coalition Poland - PSL, UED, Conservatives, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Act on Tax on Goods and Services and the Act - Bank Law. At the beginning of 2021, the VAT package, called slim VAT, came into force. It was intended to seal this tax and introduce simplifications for taxpayers. Practice has shown that not everything works as it should, and it has been recognised that further changes are needed. Their aim is to further simplify the tax treatment of goods and services by taxpayers. The VAT sealing tools introduced in recent years, including the PO-PSL government, and the related effects of the fight against VAT fraud, have allowed the verification of VAT clearing arrangements in force in some cases since 2004. Legal, IT and Technological solutions in tax settlements improve taxpayers' clearing and facilitate their verification processes by tax authorities. The introduction of new regulations in the VAT Act is also important from the point of view of Great Britain and Northern Ireland from the European Union. The withdrawal agreement sets out a transitional period ending on 31 December 2020. Currently, EU VAT rules do not apply to the UK. Consequently, there are no transactions such as intra-Community supply and intra-Community acquisition of goods and mail order sales between the EU and the United Kingdom. It was therefore considered appropriate to introduce legislative amendments which would clearly indicate how the VAT rules will be applied in relations with the United Kingdom and Northern Ireland after 31 December 2020. High Seym! The new wording of the provisions of the contested law indicates, inter alia, that the fiscus cannot demand interest from an entrepreneur who will show a tax on intra-Community acquisition or import of services only after 3 months. The current limit of 3 months will be abolished. This is the result of the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 18 March 2021 in Case C-895/19. Other changes include regulations on taxation of foreign trade, which are to be supplemented by a provision which will allow for a precise assignment of shipping or transport to a specific supply, namely a specific taxable activity. This concerns both intra-Community exports and supplies. Taxpayers who use the VAT import settlement option in the tax return will be able to correct this declaration. Moreover, the flexibility of the use of special VAT accounts by taxable persons using the split payment method is to be increased and another new provision will apply to property taxation. If the taxable person has a choice as to the application of VAT instead of a civil-law tax, he will be able to make the relevant statement to be included in the notarial act. The next amendment involves adapting the institutions so-called. relief for bad debts to EU requirements. The relief for bad debts will also be possible in the case of transactions with customers, i.e. non-business natural persons, and taxable persons exempt from VAT. The amendment proposes an extension of the time limit for the benefit of bad debts to 3 years - now there are 2 years - from the date of issue of the invoice documenting the claim, counting from the end of the year in which the invoice was issued. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The amendment also proposes to amend the provisions of the Act - Bank law, which allows the transfer of funds collected in the VAT account to another VAT account of the holder, even when VAT accounts are assigned to the taxpayer's accounts kept by different banks. At present, such transfers are only possible between the holder's VAT accounts kept in the same bank. The first Slim VAT pilot scheme, which entered into force at the beginning of 2021, responded to market demands and met business expectations. He endured unnecessary and excessive responsibilities hindering his business. In turn, new solutions for the VAT Slim 2 act largely respond to the demands of entrepreneurs in relation to the need to counter the negative socio-economic effects of the COVID-19 pandemic. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Polish Coalition - Polish People's Party, the Union of European Democrats, the Conservatives recommend that the High House adopt an amendment to this law.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u123	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! It was my honor to present the effect of the work of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on the government bill amending the Police Act and some other laws from print no. 1132. The report can be found in print No 1258. Ladies and gentlemen! It's such a bill that if it arouses any emotion, it's only positive. It is a project that has been expected for a long time, not only by officers who look after their animals that support them, i.e. dogs and horses, but also, I think, by all people who love animals. The draft law amending the Law on Police and certain other laws proposes regulations on the legal status of animals used in the various departments. The situation is this: dogs and horses are used in the police and border guard service, in the fire department and in the State Security Service - dogs, in the Armed Forces - dogs and horses and in the Prison Service - dogs. Of course, the most animals that are used for service, these four-legged officers, are at the disposal of the police - 915 dogs and 54 horses, Border Guard has 252 dogs and 8 horses, fire department - 25 rescue and service dogs, of which 15 have search specialties. Today, it is actually difficult to imagine the operation of the service, such as the police and the others I have mentioned, without the assistance of these four-legged officers. Even this hall today, before we took our seats, was tested by dogs that are at the disposal of the Marshal's Guard. It was two dogs, one already retired. There will be four dogs at the disposal of the Marshal's Guard. This law, as I mentioned, comprehensively defines the status of an animal on duty: from the moment of selection e.g. a dog for the service, through the time of carrying out tasks in the various departments, until he is withdrawn from the service. Dogs assist officers in ensuring security and peace. Dogs used by the service save lives and health in search operations, help search for drugs or other forms of contraband, and are invaluable in the search for explosives, as well as in the detection of criminals. The history of dogs and horses in the widely understood police services dates back to 1890. The first dog he ever served was called Topper. It was a foxterier, if I remember correctly, and he served in the London Police. The first police dog school was opened in Bavaria. On the other hand, as far as Poland is concerned, in 1913, i.e. before independence, in Krakow there was a doberman who served in the Police. And the first horse police squadron was established in 1920 in Łódź. So it's a pretty long story and, as I said, today it's hard to imagine the operation of the services without the help of a dog. The Act sets out the requirements for the guardian to be entrusted with the care of the animal in the various formations. It also defines very precisely the responsibilities of this caretaker, that is, how he is to take care of this dog, provide him with proper food, proper food, proper condition, proper care. The Act also lays down rules on the feeding of animals on duty, as well as rules on the control of whether this care is properly exercised. The keeper of such an animal will receive board in kind or an appropriate cash equivalent. The bill, as I said, also provides for the withdrawal of animals from the service. It is about regulating the status of withdrawn animals, i.e. how often the media, dog pensions, colloquially define them. It seems to me that animals that have served man throughout their lives deserved to be given the right conditions during the period when they were withdrawn from service when they were old and sick. Animals are withdrawn from service as a result of permanent loss of performance, non-rotational disease, lack of progress in training. The dog can be withdrawn after the age of nine and the horse after the age of 15. The law is very specific about who will be able to be the guardian of an animal withdrawn from service. The principle is that, first of all, what seems right would be the keeper of this animal. It is known that the relationship with such a person is the best, the animal simply knows it. However, it will not always be possible for such a person to take care of the animal, so the law provides exactly who can then become its guardian. If such a person does not find himself, then the caretaker can be a non-governmental organisation aimed at protecting animals, caring for animals. That's a very good solution. It is very good also for a veteran shelter. Ladies and gentlemen, there's a shelter under Poznan that's run by a retired officer, animal keeper. He created this place, but let's not hide it, it's very difficult to run this kind of shelter without any support. After the adoption of this bill, the situation will look completely different. Like I said, it's a government project, but I think if he were to influence it as a parliamentary project, it would be possible to sign it over political club divisions. I think most of them would definitely sign off on this project. Like I said, it's just a good project. It was expected until a long time ago. Amendments were tabled during the committee's work. Most of these amendments, or 49 amendments, are legislative and editorial amendments. There is no doubt about any further amendments. They specify e.g. certain definitions. There is one amendment that is very important. Minister, in this project, you missed our beloved Seym dogs. It's very good that it's been fixed. There is an amendment whereby dogs that are in the state of the Marshal's Guard will also be covered by the Act. The bill has another very good record. The commandant of a given unit will be able to apply for a dog's and horse's pension also to those animals who have completed their service before the entry into force of the Act. I hope that this good solution will benefit from the veteran shelter I mentioned earlier in Poznań. There are 17 such animals there right now. Ladies and gentlemen! Like I said, it's a good project, although it's a government project. Minister, of course, this is a joke. On behalf of my colleagues on the committee, I ask the High House to adopt this law as soon as possible. Four-legged officers and their guardians are waiting for this. Thank you very much. The Act regulates the status of animals in services supervised by the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration, i.e. in the Police, Border Guard, State Fire Department, State Protection Service and animals that work in the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland and the Prison Service. Despite significant progress, the development of new technologies, animals continue to play a significant role in assisting officials in carrying out their statutory tasks. It is the animals that provide real support for the officers, and in some cases they are irreplaceable. It was therefore necessary and socially expected to regulate the status of animals in the service. The draft law introduces the possibility of using service dogs or service horses to carry out specific statutory tasks: the protection of life, health and human property, the protection of security and public order, counter-terrorism activities, the detection of crimes and offences, the prosecution of perpetrators, the execution of representational tasks, the assurance of order and security in prisons and detentions and, finally, the protection of facilities established by the Council of Ministers. Currently, in all formations under the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration, a total of 1.2 thousand serve. dogs and over 60 horses. Annually around 10% the animals are withdrawn from the service and retire. The solutions proposed in the Act apply to animals from their selection to service, through the duration of the tasks in the various formations until they are withdrawn from service and retired. The draft law sets out the obligations of keepers of animals, including in particular those relating to ensuring the proper maintenance of animals, including: rational feeding and permanent access to clean and fresh water, care for health and condition, care, keeping the animals clean and ensuring conditions of maintenance adapted to biological needs. At the same time, a mechanism has been defined for the allocation by the different formations of animals withdrawn from the use of carers, provided that the animal continues to remain in the state of formation and that the guardian will have priority in taking care, if he so wishes. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Law and Justice fully supports the draft law, which contains ten articles. I want to make an amendment to the article at the same time. 2, which concerns animals working in the Armed Forces. The amendment includes the title that is added as follows: Dogs and horses in the Armed Forces. The amendment sets out the status of animals used in the Armed Forces. It concerns the health care of these animals and training. There are also records on diet and care, including veterinary medicine. The supervision of dogs that work in the Armed Forces is defined. The law we are discussing shall enter into force three months after the date of the announcement. However, the legislator provided for the possibility of implementing the provisions of this law earlier, so that animals withdrawn from the service could be protected even before its entry into force. The adoption of the proposed solutions is the fulfilment of social expectations (The Bell), including the expectations of the officers and soldiers themselves, so I ask the High House to adopt this law over divisions, with full approval. Thank you very much.

It applies only to rescue dogs owned by a natural person who is a member of an entity incorporated in the national emergency and fire protection system or an entity with which a civil law contract has been concluded. It should also be noted that the rules do not include animals serving in the National Tax Administration. While this service has regulated these matters in a separate regulation, it would be worthwhile to make the records the same for all dogs belonging to these services. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u165	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Indeed, it is rare that a government representative has the pleasure of hearing so many good words about the government's submission, because everyone actually supported this project and expressed it not only in their speeches but also in the vote, for which I thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen! The task of the animals in the service shall be to assist the officers in carrying out their statutory tasks. Indeed, despite significant progress and the development of new technologies, animals continue to play an important, crucial role, providing real support for officers, and in some cases they are irreplaceable. They assist in the implementation of tasks related to the conservation of human health and life in search operations, carry out detection activities related to the search for perpetrators of crime or victims of those crimes, carry out preventive measures for the detection of dangerous substances. Finally, as we should also remember, they play an important ceremonial role during various official public ceremonies. In the assessment of the management of the Ministry of Interior and Administration, the existing arrangements regarding the situation of animals in uniform formations were and are still insufficient. That is why, a few months ago, we decided to start working on comprehensive regulation of the status of animals used in the uniform services of the Ministry of Interior and Administration, both for animals used to support the implementation of statutory tasks and those - because from the beginning this idea has been guiding us - out of the service. With great satisfaction and unhindered joy, we have observed how in the subsequent stages of legislative work and even earlier - when we announced in the Ministry's first press conference that we had started such work - our proposal was accompanied by further formations. Let me remind you, the first version only concerned the regulation of the status of animals in the services of the Ministry of Interior and Administration. Each further formation aroused our great joy and satisfaction. This was complemented by what happened at the last committee meeting when, as I said, the amendment included dogs serving in the Marshal's Guard. It has been said in almost all speeches that dogs and horses serve in the Police, Border Guard, the Armed Forces, and dogs in the State Protection Service, the Prison Service and the Marshal's Guard. Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to make it clear - because this thread did not appear here, but I think it is good for it to sound out of this speaker - that this project will not exclude the application of the provisions of the Animal Protection Act. He complements it by referring to animals used for special purposes. According to Art. 4 point 20 of the said Animal Protection Act, by means of animals used for special purposes, means animals whose professional training and use are carried out on the basis of separate provisions governing the detailed rules of operation of the services and the rules of training and use of dogs. However, so far there has been a lack of separate rules - they are being introduced by this law. All the existing regulations were scattered, were in lower order acts, such as internal decisions or regulations, and in principle ignored the situation of animals after withdrawal from the service, from the use in formations. Our project proposes solutions to regulate the situation of animals from the time they are selected for service, throughout the duration of this service, until they are withdrawn from the formations. However, the Article will continue to apply, for example. 15 Animal Protection Act, laying down rules for the handling of animals used for special purposes. Ladies and gentlemen! To briefly discuss the main objectives of the project, it should be pointed out that it sets out the conditions to be met by the keeper to whom the animal is assigned in the various formations; the responsibilities of the keepers of animals; the rules for feeding animals; the rules for the treatment and financing of animals; the rules for dealing with emergency situations in which animal health or life is at risk; and, finally, the cases of withdrawal from the use of animals in the various formations; a very detailed mechanism for entrusting keepers of animals withdrawn from service - assuming, and this is a key change, that they will always remain in the state and under the care of the formation in which they have served so far. This is because one of the basic deficits of earlier, these barely residual internal regulations in the various departments concerned supervision. Now this law also lays down the rules for the supervision of the various formations of how this care for the entrusted animals withdrawn from service is exercised. Therefore, in the context of this surveillance and other issues which I have mentioned, it should be pointed out that this project is complementary to the arrangements adopted in the Animal Protection Act. Already at a very preliminary stage, even before the work on the committee, the question arose as to what happened to the animals that had previously been removed from the service. I would like to say that all such voices coming from external, internal stakeholders, from the services themselves and all the demands - we ourselves felt the need - were included in this project. The first reading was of a consistent and concerted nature, because we, in the quotes of the amendments, have already taken into account at that stage. We have just decided that, in cases justified by the welfare of animals at the request of the guardian, the commanders of certain services and formations will also be able to include those provisions also animals which were withdrawn from the service prior to the entry into force of this law. This is a key issue, especially for such institutions and social organisations, whose aim is to protect animals, as mentioned by among others. rapporteur, this organisation, this shelter, this asylum we have been talking about. Let me now refer to the conclusions and questions that have been raised by you. Firstly, thank you very much for the votes that clearly support and declare support for this law. There were several issues that were common in nature, so let me treat them and discuss them more problematicly, and I will not refer to individual questions, because most of them repeated, they were slightly different, but they really dealt with the same aspects. The first such thread, which was scrolling, was about customs and treasure. There were questions as to why this situation was not regulated. First of all, I would like to remind you once again that this was an initial project for animals used in the services of the Ministry of Interior and Administration. As I said, we were happy to accept further formations that joined our project: armed forces, prison service. On the other hand, with regard to the customs and treasury service, this thread was explained in detail by me and by representatives of the Ministry of Finance already at the committee meeting. According to Art. 64 of the National Tax Administration Act, which defines the situation of official animals used in customs and treasury checks, regulates their status and the conditions under which they remain under the care of carers by means of a regulation. This was the will, and this was the position of a specific department to maintain the rules in this way. The second thread that appeared was why the project did not include municipal guards. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, According to Art. 2 Municipal Guards Act may be set up by the municipal council and may be dissolved by the same body. It is not possible to impose additional financial obligations on municipalities, especially in the context of their optional tasks. It is the organizational unit of the municipality. The costs associated with the operation of the guard are covered by the budget of the municipality, which is mentioned in the article. 5th Municipal Guard Act. It would therefore be difficult for us to impose permanent obligations on the municipal authorities. But - I would join such an appeal - let us, from this House today, after our submission, come up with a proposal that individual local governments should regulate this matter on their own. I think that today we have the opportunity to set a good example and if individual local governments want to follow these solutions, it will be very good. Also, there was often a thread about dogs that are not owned by the State Fire Department and are not in state, on duty, and are used for rescue operations. Indeed, all contract dogs will be covered by these provisions. On the other hand, only those - and that is, in my opinion, natural in the case of such a nature of the provisions - which we propose - are permanently carrying out the tasks referred to in the Act. Ladies and gentlemen, to questions which were more individual in nature concerning funding, because this issue has also appeared several times, I answer. Funding will be within the limits in force for each formation. We have assurances - after all, this project was constantly developed with the support, with the voice of all formations, both those subordinate to the MSWiA and the others - that all services are aware that it will burden their budgets (the bell) and it is, I think, an honest situation. When someone decides to include an additional animal, they take on this cost. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to stress once again that this project meets the social expectations, expectations of the officers themselves, but not just them. All these expectations, I think, have their origin in the sense of our common ethical duty to ensure a decent existence for the animals in the service and to withdraw from the service. In view of the above, I am counting on the support of the countries that we are proposing. I believe, as I mentioned at the first reading of the project during the committee's work, that, due to social but also political acceptance, the project governing the status of animals in the services will be adopted beyond political divisions and will be an expression of our concern for the smaller brothers. Let us remember that the measure of our humanity is our attitude toward animals. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u217	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed law aims to improve and make the work of the Medical Research Agency more flexible, which - as has been done many times here - is a very important institution in terms of the development of health sciences, the development of pharmaceutical companies or the development of modern treatments for the treatment of diseases which are very complicated, rare and which have not been invented so far. The comments made regarding the selection of experts are not entirely correct given that this procedure is already described on the websites. It is not so that the failure to apply the law - Public procurement law causes experts to be chosen in a secret, unclear or uncategorized manner. There are rules, criteria for the selection of experts. Anyone who meets the requirements laid down by the Agency for Medical Research may make themselves known and participate in the selection, evaluation, project clearance or analysis work. Such rules apply not only to experts selected in the Agency for Medical Research, but also for example. in the case of European measures for which the Public Procurement Law is not applicable and is open to recruitment, the criteria are defined, the committee shall assess whether the experts meet the requirements, a list of these experts shall be drawn up and these experts shall then be publicly selected for the evaluation of the projects. In addition, experts sign statements of impartiality each time, before each project which they get to evaluate, so there is no risk that someone may not be impartial or unfair to carry out assessments. Issues concerning medical experiments are governed by the Pharmaceutical Law Act and the Act on Competitions of Doctors and Dentist. Already today, there are laws that tell us how to conduct clinical trials or medical experiments. They are primarily intended to serve the treatment, the use of modern treatment in people who suffer from very complicated diseases, rare diseases. Here we also place emphasis on cardiac and oncology diseases. This is often a chance to save lives for those for whom adequate treatment has not yet been invented. I would also like to address immediately the issue of financing the activities of the Medical Research Agency from the contribution, from a write-off from the National Health Fund. Well, these drugs also treat patients, so we can't say that we're taking care of patients. The rules on the funding of the Agency have already been adopted and, as I understand it, the Members have also voted when we have examined the Act on the Agency for Medical Research, in this respect we are not currently making any change. The only change with regard to funding is to make spending by the Medical Research Agency more flexible. The point is that the funds are spent on six-year projects. Projects are implemented by individual beneficiaries of these measures, and simply situations may occur where, for various objective reasons, project schedules will be shifted over time, they will change. They can move, change during the year. Therefore, instead of putting money into the budget, we propose that we move it to the next year, so that these projects can be fully implemented and will not have to be implemented at the expense of the next initiatives that are planned, included in the budget of the Medical Research Agency. Such a solution is adopted, for example, with regard to European measures, which also do not need to be reimbursed at the end of the year in case of non-expenditure. I would like to add that it is very important that these funds must be spent on the specific purpose for which they were originally allocated. The Agency will not be able to change the purpose of spending, and therefore the original objectives, the original objectives, are retained. Only in justified cases where the beneficiaries fail to fulfil their tasks will the appropriations be transferred to the following year. The funds for investment in the Medical Research Agency will be allocated through a targeted grant, i.e. a strict target for spending these funds. These funds come from the state budget, not from a write-off from the National Health Fund. In order for the Medical Research Agency to function efficiently, it must have information systems or computer equipment. This is the means to finance the basic functions of the Medical Research Agency, as is the case with any other institution that operates and has to bear expenditure on equipment or related to the operation of workers under normal conditions. As regards the other provisions on the right to pursue a profession, I stress that all professional authorities are in constant contact with us and are aware of the changes being made. They are the result of the provisions of the Public Documents Act which state as issuer the documents of the Minister of Health. Until now, local authorities have been the issuer, which decide to grant the right to pursue the profession and also issue the right to pursue the profession. It's always been their own job. We would like this task to be carried out in exactly the same way, so that the provision reflects the proposal that the Minister of Health should be able to authorise a specific professional body to issue a document of the right to pursue a profession in accordance with the requirements set out in the Public Documents Act. This is so important that these documents will be issued as early as 1 January and must comply with the requirements set out in the Act, mainly due to the lack of possibility of forgery or misuse by wrong persons. This is a commonly used procedure, that there is a protest in case of a negative evaluation of the project. ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u222	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the club Law and Justice towards the parliamentary draft amendment of the law on political parties, print no. 1231. High Seym! The law on political parties is from 1997 and in some respects does not agree to the current legal status. Therefore, the Members of the applicant claimed that the Article should be amended. 18 of this law, which provides for the disclosure of the political party's records, which is absolutely necessary, while the elements of this disclosure go too far. This record shall be made public in respect of the addresses of persons who are part of the party authorities and persons who are entitled to enter into property obligations on behalf of the party, as well as addresses on the lists of persons who are founders, support the establishment of the party. Therefore, the project interferes with this law and deletes the possibility of disclosure of this data. We already have completely new EU rules on the protection of personal data, and in these years our national laws have also changed very much, therefore the law, whether the draft law in a slightly modified form compared to the original, still contains three articles in the report of the Committee on Printing No. 1256. Art. 1 covers the details to be included in the records; so far they have been in the Regulation and there was no legal basis for such data to be included in the records. The scope of information, which is included in the records, has therefore been detailed. However, it is important to amend the article. 18. This is the change that says that the records, together with the texts of the statutes of the party, are public, excluding information on the addresses of natural persons' residence, both those of the persons who are members of the competent authorities, as well as the persons reporting the lot to the register. It also amended the provision which explicitly states that the public information contained in the records is to be made available in the Public Information Bulletin of the Court, where, according to the lawyers, there was indeed no legal basis before, and therefore the project was supplemented. According to the club Law and Justice deserves support and we will support it, because we believe that these changes are necessary to protect those personal data that should not be disclosed. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u246	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications about the draft law on the amendment of the law presented by the President of the Republic of Poland - Civil Code, print Nos 1125 and 1218. Marshal of the Sejm at the 30th meeting of the Sejm on 20 May 2021 pursuant to Article. 87. 2 in conjunction with Article. 90. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Extraordinary Commission for amendments to the codifications for consideration. On 9 June 2021, the Extraordinary Commission considered the above-mentioned draft. Laws. During the committee's work discussions were held and amendments were tabled. I would like to remind you that the project provides for the addition in the Civil Code of provisions according to which the immediate family of the victim, who, for example, As a result of an accident, he suffered permanent injury, and he will be entitled to compensation for a breach of his family relationship. Over the past years, the case law of the general courts has formed a dominant position that such harm is to be rectified by payment of an appropriate amount of compensation. This position has been confirmed by the case law of the Supreme Court. In line with this direction, the court may grant compensation for harm to the nearest victim who has suffered severe and lasting injury as a result of an illegal act. In judicial practice, however, cases where the actions of the immediate members of the family of the victim for payment of compensation for the break-up of the family can be legally dismissed. Such decisions are justified by the fact that compensation for broken family ties cannot be investigated or based on Article. 448 Civil Code, as they do not constitute a classic personal property within the meaning of Article. 23 Civil Code, neither on the basis of Article. 446 § 4 of the Civil Code, since this provision applies only to cases of injury and subsequent death of the injured person. The apparent discrepancy and the change in the direction of resolving very important social claims are not desirable from the point of view of the principles of a democratic rule of law implementing the principle of social justice and the principle of trust in the State and its law. It is right to pay compensation when the nearest family member has suffered serious injury to his health resulting in the inability to establish or continue his family relationship. High Izbo! The Extraordinary Commission for Codification Changes after considering the draft law at its meeting on 9 June 2021 requests the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. Thank you. [[Chairman of the session includes Deputy Marshal Ryszard Terlecki of the Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u249	Thank you very much. Now I would like to ask Barbara Dolniak, the Civic Coalition. You're welcome, my fellow Member. Out of habit, I was gonna say marshal.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u250	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! This draft law is a consequence of the legal gap that we have faced in the event of compensation of money for severe and permanent damage to the body or causing a health upset. In judicial practice, in many cases both the common courts and the Supreme Court considered that we are dealing with the personal good referred to in Article. 23 The Civil Code, and they judged, if they had accepted the basis for it, to make amends in cash, on the basis of the article. 23 Civil Codes in conjunction with Article. 448 Civil Code. However, there were judgments of both the general courts and the Supreme Court, including the last resolution of the Supreme Court, taken in the composition of seven judges, which indicated that there was no legal basis for redress in the particular case to which the bill in question relates, and this resulted in the dismissal of the action. Such a situation of divergence is unacceptable to the citizen, because it creates legal uncertainty, a situation in which the citizen is not sure how the court will settle a specific case in which he claims money, in this case a claim for compensation. The first President of the Supreme Court, Professor Małgorzata Gersdorf, pointed to the existence of a legal gap already in 2018. It was there that the issue of money compensation in the event of severe and permanent injury or the creation of a health disorder requires regulation. This bill will fill the legal gap, once passed by the Sejm and the Senate, and will allow, as in the case of the article. 446 Thus, this proposal will allow for the elimination of any doubt concerning the legal basis and the ruling of courts, both universal and Supreme Courts, where, of course, the conditions resulting from this provision are met, in order to ensure adequate monetary redress in extremely traumatic situations. Indeed, there is no doubt that the situations referred to in this bill are extremely difficult for both the injured person and the nearest family members of the injured person. We're talking about a posttraumatic coma or a vegetative state of a family member, but also about, for example. damage to the child during childbirth, resulting in a lack of possibility to establish a family relationship. Of course, we are talking about situations that are unambiguous. There can be no doubt here that this may apply to temporary and temporary situations. No, the rule is unambiguous and speaks of severe and permanent damage to the body or health disorder resulting in the inability to establish or continue a family relationship. All these conditions, which are mentioned in the provision, must exist in order for the court to decide on the appropriate redress, and in the adoption of this law a legal gap will be filled and there will no longer be any doubt which provision should be applied in a specific situation. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u252	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Left Club on the presidential draft print bill No. 1125 and the report of the committee on the work on the print project No. 1218. On the day of the accident, Catherine J. was 17 years old, a high school student. A friend who ran Renault Megane lost control of him and crashed into a tree and died a week later. He had morphine and amphetamines in his blood. The girls haven't been tested because of her severe condition. She suffered extensive injuries, and the doctors assessed her condition as vegetative. Edith B. from the moment of the traffic accident which caused her severe brain injury has been in a state of deep blackout, in a vegetative state. There is no possibility of making contact with her, she has been incapacitated completely, and with the current state of medical knowledge it is not possible to improve her health. A young mother who raises children three and four years of age alone because of a medical error gets brain swelling and then stays in a stable vegetative state. In the light of experts' opinions, the loss of a mother destructively affects children's mental development, leading to adaptive, emotional, emotional difficulties. These are just a few stories of human tragedies where the court had to decide whether or not to grant compensation to the victims of crime. You don't have to wait to wake up a comatose person to find that she and her loved ones are suffering. You don't have to and you can't wait to make amends for this suffering. Family members who are close to a post-traumatic coma or a vegetative state often suffer no less than when a loved one dies. That is why such a reparation must be a direct guarantee of the law. The granting of compensation to members of the injured family is to be possible in the event of severe and permanent injury to the body or health disorder resulting in a break-up or inability to establish family ties. This amendment provides for this submission. The proposed amendment is intended to help resolve the differences of case law that have arisen in recent years, both at the level of the general courts and the Supreme Court. Currently, the Civil Code makes it possible for the court to grant the nearest family members an adequate amount of compensation for the harm suffered only in the case of a loved one who has died as a result of an illegal act. The rules do not specify the amounts in detail. In practice, problems related to the granting of redress to family members start when a person suffers severe and permanent injury or health disorder. The proposal therefore proposes that the legal basis for such decisions be expressed directly in the following Article. 446 Civil Code. As indicated in the explanatory memorandum, the provisions would apply only to the most severe cases, such as the break-up of family ties due to post-traumatic coma, vegetative state or brain damage. Although it is impossible to value or value suffering and harm, the payment of compensation in such situations should be possible, and this possibility should be guaranteed by law. Importantly, this amendment may apply not only to events that occurred only after the change of regulations. Family members whose claims will not be renewed could also request payment of compensation if they meet these criteria. Given the social importance of the problem, given that no changes were made during the committee's work to change the applicant's intention expressed in the draft submitted, the Left Club maintains its position expressed at first reading and considers that these solutions deserve support. Thank you very much.

The bill entered the Sejm on 25 March this year. as an initiative of the Legislative Commission. On 20 May, the Sejm held its first reading of the draft law, and then your Marshal addressed the draft to the Extraordinary Commission on changes in codification, which in turn referred the draft to her Permanent Sub-Committee on the revision of the Labour Code and the Code of Administrative Procedure. The subcommittee, after several hours of work on 8 June, adopted a report requesting the adoption of the draft in the form of a single text of the draft with amendments tabled, which was adopted by the committee on the following day, and annexed to the report of the committee presented in print No 1210. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed amendment to the provisions of the Code of Administrative Procedure aims to bring the system of law into line with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 12 May 2015 in the case under the signature of Act P46/13, in which it was decided that Article. 156 §2 of the Code of Administrative Procedure, in so far as it does not exclude the admissibility of annulment of a decision taken in breach of the law, where a significant lapse of time has occurred since the decision was taken and the decision was the basis for the acquisition of a right or an expetition is incompatible with Article. 2 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. It is crucial that the judgment referred to above is a substantive judgment relating to the narrow issue presented to the Court. As part of its work, the Extraordinary Commission for Codification Changes considered it appropriate to address the broader dimension of the constitutional court's problem in a way that ensures a balance between constitutional principles of legalism and legal certainty. At the subcommittee and committee meetings, after considering a number of amendments, it was decided to add the situation referred to in Article. 156 § Point 1 (2) in which a decision has been taken without a legal basis or gross infringement of the law, to the previous catalogue of circumstances in which annulment has taken place it is possible for up to 10 years from the date of service or publication of the decision and after which the decision is concluded only in breach of the law. An amendment introducing a new 30-year period for the examination of nullity cases was also accepted, after which the application would become inadmissible and would be rejected by the Authority. As a result of the proposed regulation on decisions taken in the period between 10 and 30 years back, it will therefore be possible to obtain a determination by the authority of the contested decision in breach of the law, but the decision cannot be repealed. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! It is clear from the rich judicial acquis of the Constitutional Court that the time limit for the reticence of final administrative decisions is not a constitutional principle, and that the limitation of time limits for challenging final administrative decisions is justified on grounds of legal certainty. In particular, the rule of law is based on the principle of protection of citizen's confidence in the State and its rights and the principle of legal security, which are binding on the legislator. Therefore, as well as assessing the importance of the issue, the committee considers it necessary to also introduce the transitional arrangements contained in the Article. 2. Undoubtedly, the amendment of the regulations covered by the project will have a direct effect on a number of proceedings in progress at the date of the entry into force of the Law. In this state of affairs, it is the legislator's task to provide for a solution to ensure a substantive settlement for entities which, within 30 years of their decision being challenged, have benefited from the possibility to challenge it and have not yet obtained a decision until the entry into force of the law, regardless of the fact that by that time it will be 30 years from the date of the judgment in question. On the other hand, it is necessary to ensure the stability of the legal system by securing it against flood of applications only to benefit from the regulation currently in force and irrespective of the substantive grounds for submitting them. It was therefore considered appropriate that any action resulting from the appeal of the decision, more than 30 years after its adoption, should be terminated by law. Undoubtedly, the term 30 years remains one of the longest terms in the Polish legal system with legal effects, such as the possibility to acquire property by sitting. It is significant that the limitation period for civil claims caused by crime or transgression has been fixed for 20 years. Therefore, the setting of a 30-year deadline for the enforcement of rights through administrative emergency proceedings by interested parties will implement the principles of the rule of law and the rule of law, while giving the basis for the durability of decisions delivered. During the committee's work we also extended the vacathio legis from the proposed 14 days to the standard adopted in the amendments to the codes 30 days and we propose that the law enter into force 30 days after the date of its announcement. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I would like to thank all the members of our committee and ministry, the ministries for their efficient and substantive work. On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission for Changes in Codifications, I would like the High Sejm to adopt the bill in print 1210. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Let's remember that we have laws on missing files. As far as cases from decades ago are concerned, there is often only a decision left in the archives, without this source material. Since after 30 years it is possible to intervene in the right of property, which is indicated as the strongest right of property, the adoption of the same deadline for challenging administrative decisions - not always having a material effect - must be considered rational and balanced.
