C-545/17. This draft law covers amendments to as many as six legal acts and concerns various consumer or general issues relating to changes e.g. on the lodging of pleadings before the court. Now it will be possible to send a pleading to the court through also other operators, other providers than the Polish Post. It is a condition for the trader to register the service provider as a postal operator in this respect. It is also a condition to issue the consignment by registered letter, but this will result in a positive outcome for the parties to the proceedings, because in a situation where the consignment is lost, which may of course happen, it will be easy to demonstrate that the consignment has been sent in due time and to the competent court. The regulations contained in this project also include solutions for certain changes in terms of time limits for the lodging of charges in order proceedings, taking into account people living in Poland, within the European Union or finally outside the European Union. A solution is also introduced which reduces the amount of the charge up to a maximum of PLN 750 when the consumer is sued. Finally, the draft contains a solution to the obligation that the court hearing the case should assess each time the contract on the basis of which the claim against the consumer was secured by a note in respect of the existence of prohibited clauses in such a contract. The trader will be obliged to serve together with the main contract application, which was secured by a security note. It will also be possible to clarify the existence on the applicant's part of the right to seek reimbursement of the costs of translation of the necessary documents justifying the application for aid in proceedings conducted in the Republic of Poland under the Law of 17 December 2004 on the law on assistance in civil proceedings conducted in the Member States of the European Union and on the law of assistance in order to settle the dispute before the initiation of such proceedings. These solutions, aimed mainly at the consumer, are intended to improve the consumer's situation in legal proceedings and as such to improve this procedure. Unfortunately, we do not know the next amendment led by the majority club operating in the current Sejm, and it is therefore difficult to refer to it, but we will meet it tomorrow at the emergency committee meeting and then we will be able to assess the validity of this amendment. Let us hope that it will be included as a whole in this bill (the bell) so that there will not be a time when, although the project is adopted by all the parliamentary clubs, there is a solution that does not receive such acceptance. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u295	Thank you. Catherine Ueberhan, Left.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u296	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I am pleased to reiterate the position of the Left Club on the Government Draft Print Act No 1222 and the report of the Print Committee No 1291 on the draft submitted. The draft law amending the law - Code of Civil Procedure and certain other laws is intended to implement the law of the European Union. The mandatory review of the contract, not just the notes, the longer period foreseen for appeal and the lower court fees to strengthen citizens in disputes with traders and to increase consumer protection against the activities of large companies, including banks - such changes are envisaged by the government's draft of the Code of Civil Procedure and some other laws. The primary objective of the amendment is to implement the judgments of the Court of Justice of the European Union in the so-called case. Prescription proceedings in disputes against consumers. In accordance with the indications of the Court of Justice of the European Union, national civil courts, including the referenda ruling therein, the amended provisions provide for the possibility to review the contract which the document submitted to the court and the annexes provided for, even before issuing the order for payment. All in order to capture prohibited contractual terms. It should be borne in mind that, when issued at a sitting in secret to the defendant's department, and even without his written position, an unlawful order for payment at the time of issue constitutes a enforceable security title without giving him a clause, which is almost as severe as the court's occupation for the defendant. The project also extends the ban on the transfer of rights from the consumer's bill to other persons, e.g. debt collection companies, and includes not only consumer credit so far, but all consumer contracts with the trader. Such protection is intended to cover not only the debtor himself, i.e. the side of the contract concluded with the entrepreneur, but also the guarantors. The plaintiff will have to submit a security note contract to the court. Without this, the court will not issue the order and will refer the case to a normal trial, so the defendant will be given the opportunity to present his claim before the final judgment is given. In specific cases, the defendant's statement in the court itself that he is a consumer, although fined up to PLN 300, is also sufficient. This amendment also harmonises the time limits for appeal against the order for payment, so-called. charges, up to 1 month if the debtor is in the European Union and 3 months when the service is to take place outside the European Union. In order to avoid complications, deadlines are meant for both consumers and entrepreneurs. The new solutions will also allow for the delivery of a pleading in accordance with the above terms in the form of a package recommended at a post office of any universal service provider. The charge on the order for payment, which was the subject of a claim against the consumer, will not exceed PLN 750 as submitted. That's much less than the usual procedure. The existing provisions shall apply to notes issued before the date of entry into force of this Law. The project adapts the Polish rules to the judgments of the Court of Justice of the European Union which have taken place on the basis of cases brought from Poland and Germany. The proposed solutions will increase the legal protection of citizens. Consumers and other parties to civil proceedings will be able to benefit from a comparable level of protection of their rights throughout the European Union. The changes made during the committee's work were mainly editorial and legislative and did not raise any objections and were supported by the committee. If the amendment tabled now at second reading does not affect the nature and records of the submission, then club The Left will continue to support this submission so far. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u318	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission for Energy, Climate and State Assets at its meeting which took place today considered a government bill amending the Power Market Act and some other laws. The Commission adopted amendments mostly of a legislative nature. There were also substantive amendments, extending the original scope of the Act somewhat, allowing electricity generators and power to adapt to the changing rules. After first reading, the Commission recommends that the attached draft law be adopted together with the amendments. A few words about the project itself. The proposed amendments result from the adaptation of Polish law to the provisions of the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 5 June 2019 on the internal market in electricity. The provisions of that Regulation entered into force on 4 July 2019 and directly on the basis of them, the first capacity auction in 2020. The Polish power market was approved by a decision of the European Commission on 7 February 2018. According to the market regulation, existing regulations on capacity mechanisms, such as the power market in Poland, should be adapted to the provisions of the above mentioned regulation. regulation, and that is what we are trying to do here. The market regulation lays down rules to ensure the functioning of the internal energy market and environmental policy requirements, including CO emission limits From the perspective of the power market in Poland, the key issue is to regulate at the level of national legislation the share of the power market, from 1 July 2025 onwards, of units emitting more than 550 g of carbon dioxide from fossil fuels into the Kilovatohour of electricity produced. Ladies and gentlemen! In view of these issues, the purpose and purpose of these legislative and legislative works is to introduce and achieve results such as adapting the provisions of the law to the market regulation, eliminating uncertainty on the part of market participants in terms of its functioning after the entry into force of the market regulation in the context of its direct application, identifying solutions for how to take account of emission limits in the power market processes and clarifying interpretation doubts and improving the functioning of the power market processes. Finally, High Sejm, Mr Marshal, I would ask that the opposition parliamentarians, who will try to speak on this issue, separate energy security issues from policy issues, because unfortunately, among these substantive votes at the committee meeting, as well as after the committee meeting, there are those which had nothing to do with the facts. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u320	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Among the most important solutions in the revision is compliance with EU environmental policy requirements. It goes on, among other things. to waive the admission to market share of the capacity of high-carbon units, i.e. emitting more than 550 g CO2 per kWh and on average less than 350 kg CO2 per kWh per year. At today's meeting of the Committee on Energy, Climate and State Assets and at the first reading of the law, the members of the governing coalition proposed two amendments. They strongly stressed that production units currently emitting more than 550 g CO The amendment clauses in the Act also introduce a new formula for the calculation of the power charge for consumers with the so-called. a stable energy consumption profile, such as those that do not generate peak peak demand spikes. High Izbo! I would like to remind you that in 2018 a power market was introduced in Poland, which resulted in the transition from the single commodity market, only energy, to the dual commodity market, energy and power. The solutions were intended to modernise a significant part of the generation assets needed for the proper balancing of power in the national electricity system and to build new blocks. The current provisions of the Power Market Act are intended to motivate new market participants, among others. investors planning to build energy storage facilities. They are designed to strengthen the security of electricity supply and reduce the risk of so-called. Blackout, because as we know, the most expensive energy is the undelivered one. As the practice shows, the costs of the power market are lower for business than the potential losses associated with the risk of interruptions in electricity supply, which may generate millions of losses resulting from each lack of energy. Associated with the introduction of the so-called "power market'. the capacity levy, which has been added to the energy consumers' accounts since January 2021, is an investment in the construction of new ones and the modernisation and maintenance of existing production units, as well as support for power plants to adapt to environmental standards. In the draft amendment to the Power Market Act, a new formula for calculating the power charge was proposed by the European Commission. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Proceded solutions are a response to requests made by entrepreneurs, especially by so-called. energy-intensive customers. Importantly, they apply to all recipients and are therefore not subject to allegations of unlawful use of State aid. The new proposal to charge capacity does not require notification to the European Commission, will contribute to the stability of the electricity system, will reward stable energy consumption and consumption in honorable hours and will allow consumers to reduce their energy charges. The law is to enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, with the exception of Article. 6 which will enter into force on 1 July 2024. ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u434	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! A nation that does not respect its past does not deserve respect for the present and has no right to the future," said Marshal Józef Piłsudski. 76 years ago, on June 21, 1945, 16 leaders of the Polish Underground State with General Okulicki heard the verdict handed down in a show trial in Moscow. After the unprecedented kidnapping by the NKVD in March 1945, the leaders of underground Poland were transported to Moscow and imprisoned in Lubianka. For the next three months, they were intensively interrogated in preparation for the demonstration process. The trial began on 18 June, following Soviet models, which obviously had nothing to do with the judicial process known for its democratic principles. It was a manifest violation of international agreements which do not recognise the judgment of the State authorities of one State by the judicial authorities of another State under its laws. In an indictment based on the Soviet penal code Poles were accused of illegal activity in the National Army, the Council of Ministers and the Council of National Unity and the preparation and execution of acts of diversion and killing of members of the Soviet armed forces, maintaining illegal radio stations in the rear of Soviet troops and committing other criminal acts. And in addition, General Okulicki was accused of espionage and conducting an interview at the rear of the Red Army. At the end of the day, the Attorney General. Afanasyev gave an accusatory speech in which he outlined a Stalinist vision of Poland's history. He stated that the process summarized the criminal activity of the Polish reaction, which fought against the Soviet Union over many years and sold the interests of its people. The indictment was therefore a clear statement that the Polish authorities independent of Moscow are illegal, and the future Poland is to be a country dependent on the USSR. 21 June 1945 at 3 p.m. 16.30 General Vasily Ulrich read the verdict issued by the Military College of the Supreme Court of the USSR. Twelve defendants were sentenced to prison from 4 months to 10 years. General Leopold Okulicki was sentenced to 10 years, Jan Stanisław Jankowski for 8 years, Adam Bień for 5 years. Three were acquitted, and Anthony Pajdak was sentenced to five years in prison in a separate trial. Three arrested never returned to Poland. General Leopold Okulicki, Jan Stanisław Jankowski and Stanisław Jasiukowicz died in prison. Fourth - Kazimierz Póżak died in prison after returning to Poland. After the punishment, some of the convicts returned to the country, and some went into exile. High Seym! Hello and praise to the heroes who gave their lives for free Poland. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u435	Thank you very much. Mrs Anna Dąbrowska-Banaszek, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u436	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! On June 23, 1943, the second stage of the displacement and pacification action began in Zamość. The Werwolf action - the Werewolf action - was carried out by the Germans in June and August 1943. During that period, 30 and 60 thousand were displaced. Poles from 171 villages. Resettlements within the Werwolf action were the most brutal actions of the German administration in this area and were intended to completely clear the area from Poles under German and Ukrainian colonization. The action was a practical implementation of the Nazi plan of Generalplan Ost, which was to gain a living space for the Germans. Its aim was to completely expel the Polish population from Zamość region and settle it with Germans. The action was the third attempt by the German colonization of these areas during the occupation. The villages inhabited by the Germans were also to be surrounded by a belt of villages inhabited by Ukrainians and Volksdeutschs. They were supposed to be a protection zone, separating the race of masters from possible attacks by Polish guerrillas. The operation was on a wide and unprecedented scale in occupied Poland. Two Wehrmacht reserve divisions, a battalion of seven police regiments, horse police divisions, motorized military battalions, the Ostlegion battalions, and even the aviation squadron took part in the action. The operation involved large forces of military police, SS, auxiliary Ukrainian police and a total of about 10,000. Wehrmacht soldiers. During the Werwolf action, there were many murders, robberies, and rapes against the civilian population. The executions as a tool of mass terror were carried out in the so-called. Zamojska Rotunda, where about 8,000 were shot. persons. In total, 30 and 60 thousand were displaced. persons from 171 villages in the powiats: Bilgorajski, Tomaszowski, Zamość and Hrubieszowski. Most of them went to concentration camps, among others. in Lublin, Majdanek. 4454 children were transferred to the Third Reich, where they were Germanized. The German occupation authorities conducted in the years 1941-1943 the displacement of the Polish population from the Zamość region was part of a large-scale plan for the new colonization occupied by the Germans in the areas of Central and Eastern Europe. Between 1939 and 1944, some 110 thousand were displaced from these areas. Poles from 297 villages. We remember. That's our story. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u450	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! 8 years ago, on 23 June 2013, the salt mine in Bochnia was included in the UNESCO World Heritage List. Thus, the uniqueness and natural and unique interior of the Bochean soup were appreciated. It is the oldest mining plant in Europe, which operated continuously from 1248 until the beginning of the 1990s. 20th century In the early days, as a royal company, he was making huge profits. It should be stressed that salt was then the strategic commodity of the kingdom, which was responsible for 25% of its revenue. It was the salt that financed the Polish revolution Kazimierz later called the Great. In the 15th and 16th centuries there was a significant development of the mine, which at that time employed nearly 500 people. In the 17th century, due to war, among others. The Swedish flood, followed by the economic collapse, has stopped the company's development. After 1771, the mine and the city were in the Austrian conquest. New windows and new decks were created. The mine also operated after Poland regained its independence, although in the 20th century there was a successful reduction in salt extraction due to its cost-effectiveness. In 1981 the mine was entered in the register of monuments, while in 1990 industrial salt mining ceased. Since then, a new chapter of tourism activities has begun for this oldest mining plant. The production of salt made Bochnia one of the most important economic centers of medieval Małopolska. Almost every century, it had a clear impact not only on the economic development of the city and its surroundings, but also on the development of industry and transport. She was an important driver of spatial, functional, social and cultural transformations. The lift towers of the shafts of Campi, Sutoris and Trinitatis, as well as other objects of the earth are still shaping the landscape of Bochni. Salinar history of the city is also reflected in the characteristic and original street naming. It was in the Bocheń mine that mining methods were being modernised, introducing innovative technical solutions, such as those used as extraction machines, and in 1930 a steam machine was installed in 1909 at the Laura Huta in Chorzów. You can also admire it perfectly well preserved today. Currently, the salt mine in Bochnia is a real pearl among the monuments of south-eastern Poland. It is very authentic, and in the absence of a museum character it seems like a recently completed production, which combined with multimedia holograms creates a very interesting effect. There is also a great interest in underground crossings by wooden boats, mining railway rides or a 140-metre slide. It is worth noting here that being in a unique microclimate of underground excavations reduces the symptoms of allergies and improves the health of people with respiratory diseases. Today's anniversary is an opportunity to thank all those who contributed to the inclusion of the mine in the UNESCO World Heritage List. I would also like to thank the Government of the United Right for supporting the activities of the Bochen mine so far and ask for further support, especially in the context of the ongoing pandemic, which has slowed down tourism for many months and has thus significantly reduced the revenues of the mine. ParlaMint-PL_2021-06-23-sejm-33-1.u455	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Battle of Osuch is considered one of the greatest guerrilla battles in Poland during World War II. In June 1944, the Council adopted Decision (CFSP) The Germans carried out two shares under the codenames "Sturmwind I' and "Sturmwind II'. Their aim was to eliminate the troops of the Battalions of Chłopskie and the National Army operating in the Lublin region and the forests of the Solska Forest. The result of Sturmwind II was the Battle of Osuch, also known as the Battle of Sopot, fought on June 25, 1944. Partisans effectively paralyzed German communication, prevented access to nearby villages and even larger cities, but most importantly they organized numerous and effective retaliation and combat actions. The Germans, realizing the dangers posed by the numerous guerrilla troops gathered in the Solska Forest, directed about 30,000 to fight. Soldiers, armored and aerospace weapons. The scale of the action was an event in the General Government, as never before or ever thereafter were the forces involved in the GG as in June 1944. As a result of the battle, almost the entire Polish commanding staff and several hundred AK and BCh soldiers were killed in the area. These tragic events, heroism and martyrdom of Polish soldiers commemorate among others. The cemetery in Osucha, where more than 300 dead lie. It is the largest guerrilla necropolis in Europe. It is estimated that between 375 and 700 soldiers of the Polish underground were killed in battles in the Solska Forest. We managed to identify by name, surname and nickname of ca. 430 fighters. However, these data are incomplete. This number includes guerrillas whose bodies were never found or buried nameless in the Osucha cemetery. Although the Battle of Osuch is the largest guerrilla battle in Poland, it is not spoken of much, although one of the plaques on the grave of the Unknown Soldier is dedicated to this battle. About it are silent textbooks for history, vainly looking for films telling the story of the heroic fight of soldiers of the Polish underground coming from this area. Each year on the last Sunday of June in Osucha, the village located in the Łukowa commune in the Bilgoraj district takes place commemorating the battle of 1944. The central event of the celebration is the Holy Mass in behalf of the victims, attended by loved ones and members of the fallen families, members of numerous patriotic and veteran associations and organizations, and all those who wanted to pay tribute to the fallen. In Osucha, right next to the cemetery there is a memorial house built years ago on the initiative of residents of the commune and the families of the fallen. This year, thanks to the funds acquired under the Government Local Investment Fund, the modernization of the building of the former school will begin, in which the Museum of Polish Partisans will be established, the branch of the Biłgoraj Land Museum. The choice of a building is not accidental. The former school is a pre-war monument, in its walls at one time the military police were based responsible for the detention and cruel torture of guerrillas. Thanks to the many years of efforts of representatives of the Association of Family Soldiers of the Polish Underground State, Osucha 1944, the authorities of the Łukowa commune and the Bilgoraj district using the funds obtained from the Government Local Initiative Fund, a museum will be created, which will be our thanks and tribute to the soldiers who gave their lives in the service of their homeland. May the memory of heroes and their victim never be wiped out.
